**Book 194:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas I* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-875-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 1 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (Fase A outcomes and ATP, printed pp. 1–14) plus one guide section per student chapter, with objectives, lesson flow and timing, activities, misconception notes, remedial and enrichment tasks, project rubrics, answer keys keyed to named student-book pages, and reproducible ten-frame, number-bond and game templates (printed pp. 1–200). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. The guide reproduces the student book's chapter covers, placeholder ISBN and all; its own ISBN is as given here. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 195:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 196:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas III* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Hobri, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-936-3. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 3 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table) plus one guide section per student chapter, with teaching guidance, misconception notes and answer keys; its Bab 1 answer-key tables (printed pp. 64–91) key every practice item, not only the chapter tests (printed pp. 1–230). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 197:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 198:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Two-digit numbers as tens + ones (18 = 1 ten + 8); place analysis of numbers | Armenia, Mathematics 2 Part I, Revision pp.4–7; place analysis p.16 |

| Test and consolidation: places, abacus, sequences — a test (Сорих): for the numbers 1528, 2685, 5821, 8152 answer the questions: in which number does the digit 8 express the value 8 hundreds? In which number does 5 express the value 5 tens? In the other numbers, in which place is each digit and what value does it express?; five abacuses are pictured: a. write the numbers in your notebook; b. write the numbers in a place table; c. write the numbers in the form of a sum of place addends; d. write the numbers in order from biggest to smallest; e. round each number by tens, hundreds and thousands; f. for the first number, write the numbers 1, 10, 100 and 1000 bigger and smaller than it; connect the numbers with the matching points on the number line 0-1000 (100, 250, 410, 580, 720, 860, 970); consolidation (Бататгах): observe and find the unknown numbers in the strip sequences (5318, 5328, 5338, …, 5358, …, 5398; 7152, …, 7552, 7652, 7752, …; 1123, 2123, 3123, …, 6123, 7123, …); a counting game (Тоолъё): in pairs, prepare cards numbered 2, 3, 4, 5, 10, 50, 100, 1000: a. each pupil composes and writes a one-digit number; b. shuffle the cards and lay them face down; c. each player takes one card from the pile; d. increasing (decreasing) their composed number by the number on their card, they count and say five numbers; their partner checks whether they counted correctly | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 20 |
| How many tens/hundreds in a number; digit-card games — numbers are given in the place table (6083; 3567; 1394): a. write these numbers in the form of place-addend sums; b. write the place and the value of the digit 3 in the numbers; c. write the numbers in order from biggest to smallest; d. for each number write the numbers 10, 100 and 1000 bigger and smaller than it; e. round the given numbers by tens, hundreds and thousands; worked example 4: the number 6372 — does it contain tens, hundreds and thousands? A boy and a girl answered using place-value cards: the boy cut a strip showing 6372 as the number of ones (нэгжийн тоо), 637 as the number of tens (аравтын тоо), 63 as the number of hundreds (зуутын тоо) and 6 as the number of thousands (мянгатын тоо); the girl used triangular place cards (6 thousands, 3 hundreds, 7 tens, 2 ones); compare using the abbreviations (мян = thousand, з = hundred, а = ten, н = one): 5 з □ 52 а; 19 мян □ 20 з; 3 а 2 н □ 2 а 3 н; 4 з 5 а □ 3 з 15 а; a game "Who will compose the biggest number?": a. each player prepares a place table and digit cards 0-9; b. shuffle the cards and put the pile face down; c. each player takes one card from the pile and writes the number in one cell of their place table; d. they take one more card and write the number in one of the remaining cells; they repeat this action twice more; e. compare the numbers: how should you play in order to compose this number? Continue playing; the rules can be changed | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 21 |
| Combined consolidation (Нэгдсэн бататгал) — reading, writing, comparing and rounding numbers up to ten thousand: write the numbers given in words as digits: a. write the numbers; b. write them in the form of a sum of place addends; c. establish how many ones, how many tens, how many hundreds and how many ten-thousands there are in them (a table with columns ten-thousands, hundreds, tens, ones); mark the following numbers on an undivided number line: write them in sequence from smallest to biggest (3452, 75, 705, 1979, 5050, 7300, 2907, 4000, 9998, 6001, 8100); write the green-coloured numbers in words (a number line from 0 to 10000 with the marks 254, 1802, 3000, 3980, 5500, 6100, 7506, 8050 and 9512); depict the following food products' prices with a bar diagram (price tags of 700₮, 2500₮, 350₮, 850₮, 2800₮ and 1250₮ are pictured on foods); discuss what value the underlined digit expresses (in 2793, 7900, 5001, 5605 and 1094); compare the numbers (998 □ 1000; 2998 □ 996; 1897 □ 1890; 3497 □ 3507; 3210 □ 3157; 1700 □ 2697; 2017 □ 2020; 3999 □ 8762; 2018 □ 2108); is 706 nearer to 0 or to 1000? Depict it on the number line and show it; round the numbers 3672, 4098, 5013, 7108 and 1500: a. to tens; b. to hundreds; c. to thousands, and write them; what digits can stand in the place of * for 68*4 > 6809 to hold?; count: from 8452: a. increasing by tens up to 8700; b. increasing by hundreds up to 10000; from 1950: a. decreasing by tens down to 1500; b. decreasing by hundreds down to 50; mark the numbers lying at a distance of 50 ones from 348 on the number line: explain how you found them; an activity: the pupils read the covered number in turn: check whether your partner reads it correctly: what pattern do the numbers coloured in one colour have (two charts of the numbers 901-1090 with cells coloured in patterns, one chart by a boy and one by a girl); separate out and write the numbers bigger than 1978 and smaller than 2013 (999, 3152, 2010, 2099, 1999, 909); arrange the numbers in increasing order (94, 112, 100; 276, 275, 302, 288; 810, 830, 790, 791); observe and find the unknown numbers (6318, 6328, 6338, □, 6358, □, □, □, 6398; and 9152, □, □, □, 9552, 9652, 9752, □, □) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 124–125 |
| Consolidation: digit games and rounding — find the sums and the differences of the number pairs (4732 and 4800; 2549 and 6082); a game: guess the number, in pairs: prepare the digits 3, 4, 1, 5, a place table and a pencil: the first player thinks of a 4-digit number whose digits do not repeat and composes it: the second player guesses that number: the players swap roles and play: how to guess the number the children thought of is explained with an example (the thought number 5413, written by the guesser as 5314: the first player compares place by place, saying e.g. "My thought number's hundreds are bigger than your number's"); the show tickets were numbered from 1 to 1000: the spectators whose numbers ended in 13 were chosen as the lucky spectators: write all the chosen numbers: how many spectators were chosen (10)?; find the number equal to the given combination of hundreds, tens and ones; using the digits 9, 5, 2 and 8, compose 3-digit and 4-digit numbers without the digits repeating: round the composed numbers to tens, to hundreds and to thousands; which numbers, when rounded, become 7000?; how many four-digit even numbers can be composed with the digits 3 and 4: a. read the composed numbers; b. write the numbers in the place table; c. mark the places of the composed numbers on the number line with dots; d. write for each number how many whole tens, whole hundreds and whole thousands it has; the distances from Ulaanbaatar: Ölgii sum 1709 km, Uliastai sum 1023 km, Kherlen sum 661 km, Chinggis sum 338 km, Zuunmod 43 km: a. read the numbers and arrange them in order; b. compose questions using the numbers and ask each other; count the numbers 4372, 3392, 5032, 5428, 6777, 6020 and 7001 by increasing and decreasing them by 10, by 100 and by 1000 in turn (say three numbers for each); a self-check: the numbers 7499, 154, 509, 480, 73, 18: a. write them in words and find the digits' values; b. break them into a sum of place addends; c. round them to tens, to hundreds and to thousands | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 126 |
| Grade-4 revision: numbers to 10,000 — Chapter 1 opens with revision (Давтах): read the numbers and write the place value of the underlined digit (450; 12341; 45093; 20341; 9403; 2013); observe the pattern and say the next three numbers mentally (476, 486, 496, 506, …; 2504, 2404, 2304, 2204, …; 4232, 4332, 4432, 4532, …; 9641, 8641, 7641, 6641, …); the first-grade pupils planted 140 flowers and the second-grade pupils planted 115 more flowers than the first grade, and the third-grade pupils planted as many flowers as the first and second grades' pupils together: how many flowers did the three grades' pupils plant in all (790)?; write in decreasing order (641, 1112, 1423, 541, 453, 86, 10999; and 10985, 10895, 10885, 20985, 20185, 10805); calculate in your notebook following the table (△ + 225 for △ = 200, 125, 251, 3750, 45075, 3256); find the pattern and complete it (three number pyramids whose bricks add upward); find the expressions' values (350 + 150; 153 + 607; 350 − 250; 953 − 307; 350 × 10; 19 × 5; 23 × 4; 29 × 9); observe the tables and write the fitting values (function tables: 23 × △ + 9 and 72 × △ − 324 for △ = 28 and 79; and △ × ■ + 242 and △ + 2 × ■ for the pairs (56, 24) and (74, 43)); find the expressions' values (three branching calculation diagrams combining pairs of numbers with +, ×, − and ÷); do the following calculations using the map of aimags with road distances (a. travelling from Arkhangai through Zavkhan to Khovd aimag, how many kilometres is it?; b. travelling from Bayankhongor through Ömnögovi to Dundgovi aimag, how many kilometres is it?); express in the given units (345 cm = □ m □ cm; 2 m 13 cm = □ cm; 4000 ml = □ l; 3 m 34 cm + 75 cm = □ m □ cm; 5 kg = □ g; 2343 g = □ kg □ g; 3 l = □ ml; 5 kg 340 g + 750 g = □ kg □ g); the 12 pupils of the class register their favourite seasons in the table and depict the results with a bar diagram (a registration grid of the four seasons against pupil numbers 1-12, and a summary table): discuss following the questions (a. which season do the pupils like most?; b. what is the difference between the numbers of pupils who like summer and who like winter?; c. how many pupils chose spring and autumn in all?) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 4–5 |
| Numbers up to a million: classes and places — reading, writing and comparing numbers up to a million (Сая хүртэлх тоог унших, бичих, жиших): some aimags' numbers of livestock are registered: the number of cattle 182965 is written in the place-class table and read: in which place is each digit, and what value does it express (a map of Mongolia shows the aimags' livestock numbers)?; the place-class table: the thousands class (мянган анги) has the hundred-thousands place, the ten-thousands place and the thousands place; the ones class (нэгжийн анги) has the hundreds place, the tens place and the ones place; the digits of 182965 stand in the places 1, 8, 2, 9, 6, 5 with the values 100000, 80000, 2000, 900, 60 and 5; a boy's note: "Each class has 3 places"; reading the place-value cards (100000, 80000, 2000, 900, 60 and 5): 182965 is read "one hundred eighty-two thousand nine hundred sixty-five"; writing: 182965; as a sum of place addends: 182965 = 100000 + 80000 + 2000 + 900 + 60 + 5; a girl's note lists the number as 1 hundred-thousand, 8 ten-thousands, 2 thousands, 9 hundreds, 6 tens and 5 ones; form the numbers from their place-value cards and write them as sums of place addends (738900; 15453; 982166; 100301; 150002; 38790); read the number and write it in digits (a. thirty-six thousand two hundred forty-six; b. two hundred fifty-nine thousand seven hundred seven); read the numbers and write them in words (690002; 400439; 725651; 44704; 1789; 998757); read the numbers in the place-class table and complete the numbers' structure (the table now has a millions class (саяын анги) with a millions place, then the thousands class and the ones class; rows of digits are given to read off); the numbers 999998 and 999999 are read in words in bold type, and then: 1000000 — one million (нэг сая) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 7 |
| Digit values; counting by place units — what place value does the underlined digit express (345671; 43878; 861113; 908851; 872; 299898)?; write each number in the form of a sum of its place addends (the place-class table gives the digits of 738611 as the worked example: 738611 = 700000 + 30000 + 8000 + 600 + 10 + 1; then rows of digits for 378020, 981106, 100990 and 542769); the digit 8 expresses what place value in each number (208; 4803; 38254; 56989; 89695; 802735)?; write the fitting number (87000 = 80000 + □; 35090 = 30000 + 5000 + □; 205768 = 200000 + 5000 + 700 + □ + 8; 986352 = 900000 + □ + 6000 + □ + 50 + 2); read the number and write the next two numbers in your notebook (500000, 600000, 700000, □, □; 695000, 696000, 697000, □, □; 989600, 989700, 989800, □, □; 1000004, 1000003, 1000002, □, □); which numbers the underlined digits express: form and read other numbers by circling one entry in each row of a table of thousands (1000-9000), hundreds (100-900), tens (10-90) and ones (1-9); how many ones are there in each number (78 ones = 78; 78 tens = 780; 78 hundreds = □ ones; 78 thousands = □ ones; 78 ten-thousands = □ ones; a reference box gives: 1 one = 1, 1 ten = 10, 1 hundred = 100, 1 thousand = 1000, 1 ten-thousand = 10000, 1 hundred-thousand = 100000; then: 51 tens = □ ones; 101 tens = □ ones; 47 ten-thousands = □ ones; 2 hundred-thousands = □ ones); a game: guess the number, using 20 number cards with the digits 0-9: a. two pupils in turn draw from the 10 cards and make the biggest 5-digit number; b. let your friend guess the number you formed (the example: with the drawn card 97301, help your friend by saying things like "the thousands-place digit is 9, the ones-place digit is 1"); c. the one who guesses the number gets a point; d. swap roles with your friends and play | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 8 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 53 countries (Egypt, Armenia, Jordan, Kazakhstan, South Africa, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, Sierra Leone, Libya, Mauritania, Vietnam, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Brazil, Azerbaijan, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria)**.**. Egypt carries the full climb — making groups of ten, tens and ones and the hundred chart in Primary 1 (Japanese-collaboration series), ones, tens and hundreds in Primary 2 with decomposing in the old Discover book and expanded form (402 = 400 + 2) in the new series, three-digit place value in Grade 3, big numbers to the milliards in Grade 4 (the Primary 1–3 rows were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Armenia carries tens and ones in Grade 2 and number classes to millions in Grade 3. Jordan carries place value from Grade 1 ones and tens through Grade 3 thousands and Grade 4 hundred-thousands to Grade 5 millions (the Grade 3 thousands and Grade 5 millions rungs were stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Kazakhstan carries place-value components with graphic models in Grade 2. South Africa carries hundreds, tens and units boxes in Grades 2–3. Other read countries teach place value inside their own Grade 2–3 books and join by merge.** Laos carries place value from tens-and-ones to 100 in Grade 1, through hundreds, expanded forms and numbers to 100000 in Grades 2–3, to millions and billions in Grade 5. Yemen carries place value from ones and tens in Grade 1 through expanded form to 1000, 10,000 and 999,999, then to hundreds of millions and billions. Indonesia carries place value from tens and ones in Grades 1–2, through three-digit place value in Grade 3 and place value to 10,000 in Grade 4, to ten-thousands and then to 1,000,000 in Grade 5. Nicaragua carries place value from tens and ones to 100 in Grade 1, through the hundreds table and expanded form to 1,000 in Grade 2 and to 10,000 in Grade 3, to the hundred-thousands table in Grade 4. Ethiopia carries place value from tens and ones in Grade 1, through 249 as 2 hundreds + 4 tens + 9 ones in Grade 2 and place value to 10,000 in Grade 3, to place value of 6-digit numbers in Grade 4. South Sudan carries place value from tens and ones in Grade 1 (12 = 1 ten, 2 ones), through hundreds, tens and ones with 734 in Grade 2 and the place-value table to thousands with expanded form 319 = 300 + 10 + 9 in Grade 3, to ten-thousands in Grade 4, hundred-thousands with 583,216 worked in Grade 5, and place value in groups (millions/thousands/ones) to a million in Grade 6, with place value again in Secondary 1. One book slip is never taught as fact: the Grade 4 book asks for 3647 in words but answers 3467 — teach the 3467 naming only. Iraq carries place value from tens and ones to 99 in Grade 1, through place value to 999 in Grade 2 and to 9999 in Grade 3, to place value within millions in Grade 4, and to place value within billions in Grade 5. Bangladesh carries place value from tens and ones in Class 1, through hundreds, tens and ones with thousands in Class 2 and place value up to Ayuts in Class 3, to lac, nijut and crore with South Asian commas in Class 4, and the decimal number system and place value in Class 6. Nepal carries place value from tens and ones in Grade 1, through place value to 1000 with blocks, abacus and expanded form in Grade 2 and to 9,99,999 with lakh in Grade 3, to crore in Grade 4 and 10 crore in Grade 5, and whole-number place value again in Grade 6. Cambodia carries place value from tens and ones in Grade 1 to 10 digits in Grade 6, with decimal place value in Grade 6. Kuwait carries place value up the whole ladder — tens and ones to 100 in Grade 1, expanded form of 3-digit numbers in Grade 2, 6-digit numbers in Grade 3, millions and decimal tenths and hundredths in Grade 4, and whole-number and decimal place value again in Grades 5 and 6. North Macedonia carries place value at Grade 1 (2022) — numbers to 20 and 30 taken apart into tens and ones, and counting by 10s to 100. Iran carries place value through its whole primary series — tens bundles and the tens-and-ones table in Grade 1, building through Grades 2 and 3, to millions in Grade 4 and billions in Grade 5.

Vietnam carries place value up the primary ladder — two-digit numbers in Grade 1; units, tens, hundreds, thousands and three-digit numbers in Grade 2; four- and five-digit numbers in Grade 3; six-digit numbers with place rows and the millions class in Grade 4 — and how natural numbers are written in Grade 6. Stocked 2026-11-11 by the Vietnam re-verification sweep merge.

Mauritania’s place value runs with its counting ladder — tens-and-ones language from 11–20 in Grade 1, grouping by tens in Grade 2 (printed p. 64), additive decomposition in Grade 3 (printed pp. 18, 65), explicit place value — tens, hundreds, thousands — with decomposing and composing in Grade 4 (printed pp. 37, 39), the bands to 999,999 in Grade 5, large natural numbers in Grade 6, and Entiers naturels in 1AS (established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, which found this lesson stocking no Mauritania row at all).

Libya carries place value from Grade 1 (to 20) through Grade 5 (to ten million), and again in Grade 7, Chapter 1 (stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep).

Ghana also contributes to this lesson as curriculum, teacher and student-workbook (task-sheet) evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook — and Ghana is not counted in the taught-in figure above. (Ghana rows stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.)

China carries place value through the primary books — composition, reading and writing to 100 in Grade 1 (including the place-and-think counter activity), and to 10,000 with the abacus and expanded form in Grade 2.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries place value through the primary climb — tens and ones in Grade 1, hundreds in Grade 2, to 100,000 in Grades 3–4 and to a billion in the Urdu-medium Class 5 book — with decimal place value in Class 7 and binary, octal and hexadecimal place values in the Class 12 statistics book's computers chapter.

Syria carries place value grade by grade — the number 10 as one whole ten and ten-rods and ones-cubes and the place-value table in Grade 1, hundreds and the expanded form in Grade 2, four places in Grade 3, five places in Grade 4, periods of three to the millions in Grade 5 and to the billions in Grade 6, and digit-value work into the billions in Grade 7.

Saudi Arabia builds place value one place per grade — tens in Grade 1 Part Two, hundreds and the expanded form in Grade 2 Part Two, digit-value in a table and the periods in Grade 3, and numbers within millions in Grade 4 — with the older 1445 Term-3 volumes witnessing the Part Two chapters. Kuwait gains further practice evidence from the teacher-made Grade 3 booklet misfiled in Saudi Arabia's record: block, abacus, word-form and expanded-form exercises on numbers to 1000 (PDF pp. 3–12, 35–42), cited as a Kuwaiti school booklet, not as a textbook.

Rwanda teaches place value up the whole primary ladder — hundreds, tens and ones to 999 in Year 2, on to 10,000 in Year 3, to 100,000 in P4, to 1,000,000 in P5 and beyond 1,000,000 in P6 — and returns in Senior 1 with large natural numbers and decimal place value in expanded form.

Sudan builds place value in Grade 1 — tens and ones on the abacus and place chart, in blocks to 100 — then the hundreds and numbers to 1000 in Grade 2, four-digit numbers in Grade 3, and numbers to hundreds of thousands and to millions in Grade 4.

India carries place value through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Classes 1–3 and 5 and Mathematics Classes 6–8 — from bundles of ten and ten-and-ones tables in Class 1, through hundreds, tens and loose ones, to lakh and crore in the Indian system beside the international system.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, V (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5): numbers 11-13 as ten and ones; tens and ones to 20; two-digit numbers.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6) and *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7). From its books: from Grade 1: tens — counting by grouping in tens: 3 groups of 10 cherries = 3 decenas = 30; 1 decena and 7 units = 17; from Grade 4: millions — news figures 6.072.522, 1.636.999, 7.709.521 (their sum checks); the new place unidad de millón; the cartel de valor chart CM DM UM C D U; from Grade 7: decomposition — Polynomial (expanded) decomposition of a number: 3.476 = 3·10³ + 4·10² + 7·10¹ + 6·10⁰, digit by digit from the place chart.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: numbers 11-20 as one ten and ones: bundles and singles (Lesson 21); ones and tens: 10 ones = 1 ten; numbers 11-15 in numerals and words; reading and writing numbers 11-20: numeral and word table; numbers 21-25: ones and tens with bundles (Lesson 31); numbers 30-34: ones and tens (Lesson 32); numbers 40-43: ones and tens (Lesson 33); ones and tens practice: read bundles and singles; ones and tens practice with counters and ten-frames; round tens to 90; one more and one less; the number between; reading and writing two-digit numbers: tens and ones of given numbers; from Grade 4: review: place value of numbers to 100,000 (Lesson 1); classes (periods) of numbers; reading and writing numbers to a milliard (Lesson 7); expanded form of numbers (Lesson 8). The Grade 1 Dari edition's own differing exercise items are stocked as Dari-edition rows above.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Tens and ones: begin splitting two-digit numbers into tens and ones and back; 15 = 1 ten and 5 ones, 27 = 2…; 10 more or 10 less: count on/back in whole tens to find 10 more or less than a number under 100 on the…. from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Writing and reading large numbers (to 6 digits); classes and orders; Numbers in standard and expanded form; place value of a digit. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Digits in numbers above a million; decimal place value (1–2 decimal places) (plans 4–5).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 8: Los números reales.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Writing and reading multi-digit numbers; from Grade 6: Revision of Grade 5: natural numbers, reading/writing multi-digit numbers, place-value…; Solving problems with the decimal structure of numbers: ab = 10a + b, abc = 100a + 10b +…; from Grade 7: Decimal place-value expansion of two- and three-digit numbers (ab = 10a + b, abc = 100a +…

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part One: 10 on the abacus and the place board: 1 ten and 0 ones; represent 11–19 on the abacus and place board (ones/tens); represent 20 on the place board; components of 20; from Grade 1 Part Two: place value to 20: ones and tens on the abacus and place board; place and place value of each digit (in 16: 6 ones = 6, 1 ten = 10; in 20); expanded form: a teen number as tens + ones; ones/tens splits and expanded form in the twenties (21 = 20 + 1, 24 = 20 + 4); write two-digit numbers from ones/tens (6 ones + 2 tens); digit place value revisited (7 in 71 = 70).

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires the value and/or place value of digits in numbers up to 999; Class 2 requires numbers up to 9,999 in words, symbols and expanded form, with the value of any digit; Class 3 requires value and place value up to 99,999, with expanded form; Class 4 requires the value of any digit in a seven-digit number. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: recall two-digit numbers as tens and ones: count pictured cars/chicks and split the count into tens and ones; group a counted set of pencils into tens to make counting easier; model the grouped count with base-ten blocks; expand (çözümleme) 123: first into tens and ones, then into hundreds, tens and ones; the hundred block is ten ten-blocks joined; expanded form of three-digit numbers modelled with base-ten blocks (142 = 100 + 40 + 2; then 190, 158, 184); hands-on expansion: choose a three-digit number, build it with up to 200 beans in pairs, write its expansion, add a partner's beans and expand the new total; read a number off grouped base-ten blocks and write it three ways: as a sum of blocks, in expanded form, and as a numeral (300 + 60 + 4 = 364); place names and place values in 235 and 450: ones/tens/hundreds places; a digit's place value is what it is worth in its place (5, 30, 200; 0, 50, 400); expand given numbers (147, 520, 402, 246) and write place name and place value of every digit (251, 403, 530); rebuild a number from given place values (300, 40, 2); pair game on a wipe-clean sheet in a plastic wallet: partner says a number up to three digits, write it and show each digit's place name and value, check, wipe, swap; place-value riddles: find the modelled number then change its digits as instructed; find a burst balloon's number from three place-value clues (hundreds value 300, tens value 4×5, ones value 3+5 → 328); a flower-petal number meeting three place-value conditions at once.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: the counting basin model, reading a six-digit number place by place (352,862) — its Step 6 place-name slip taught corrected; place value of each digit in numbers up to 1,000,000; the total (face) value of a digit; expanded form; abacus and basin representations of large numbers.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 3, Part 1 (student); Mathematics 4 (student); Mathematics 5 (student): writing a number as a sum of place-value addends; multi-digit numbers; classes and places of positional writing; digits; the decimal system for writing natural numbers; places and place units.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-evidence item from 1 of the guides (Book 2; Grades 2).

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. Same collection and July-2020 revised programme as Book 1; items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where the sommaire prints them, later lessons cited by sequence (seq.). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| The numbers 0 to 999, second stage (Monde des Maths 2 guide, lesson 5) | Morocco — *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), Grade 2* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, printed p. 89 |

Morocco enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Morocco's in-hand set is a teacher guide or teacher edition, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Morocco's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 32 guides are read and stocked, but no pupil book is in hand.

Placements: liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/maths/morocco-placements.md.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (6 from student books, 0 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S2, Book S3, Book S5, Book S6.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 18 items (10 from pupil books, 8 teacher evidence) from Book S3, Book S5, Book S6, Book S9, Book S10, Book S14, Book S16, Book T17, Book T25, Book T37, Book T39, Book T40, Book T42, Book T44, Book T46.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 10 items (1 from pupil books, 9 teacher evidence) from Book S13, Book T8, Book T9, Book T34, Book T35, Book T36, Book T37, Book T38, Book T39, Book T40.

Fifty-two lessons in three terms; Revision Exercises A–C and Answers are apparatus. Pages cited are printed pages.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 11 items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 5, Book 7, Book 9, Book 10, Book 12.

**Book 1:** *Escuela Nueva — Matemáticas, Grado 1, Cartilla 1* (student book, Grade 1, Primera Cartilla) — Colombia, Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN); 14,426,196 bytes, 100 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (contents-page basis, matching the printed folios).

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 teacher-evidence items from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (1r) positional value of digits in a decimal positional system; zero | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 24 |
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (1r) additive decomposition of small numbers, mentally and in writing | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 25 |
| Andorra's Primera ensenyança programme requires: (2n) expressing numbers in the positional decimal system in complex form | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 25 |

Andorra enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Andorra's in-hand set is an official curriculum programme, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 10 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4 and Book 5.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Manual da 1.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 1) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Editora Moderna, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-267-0; 4,293,873 bytes, 81 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 2:** *Matemática — Manual da 2.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 2) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-297-8; 3,437,647 bytes, 56 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 23 calls the minuend “diminuendo ou aditivo” — “aditivo” is wrong for subtraction; the course teaches minuend. Editorial production notes are printed as lesson content on printed p. 50 — production matter, cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 8 requirement-evidence items from Book 1 and Book 2.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide, Grade 6) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 868,323 bytes, 51 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–15; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 16–45. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series (M1.16 onwards). The detailed sections of this guide are markedly thinner than in Books 1–2 (several state “Specific Content: N/A”); placements rest on the outcomes as stated. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Expressing numbers up to 3 digits in usual and expanded form, naming the number 10 or 100 more or less than a given number, and that each place is 10× the place to its right (M2.3) | Belize — *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed pp. 12, 19 |
| Using place value to recognise the difference between two numbers — worked example: 8,537 is 400 less than 8,937 because the hundreds places differ by 4 (M2.9) | Belize — *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed pp. 12, 18 |

Belize enters this lesson as teacher-guide evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every document in Belize's in-hand set is a Ministry teacher's guide (Infant 1, Standard 1, Standard IV; August 2012), not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as pupil teaching (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Belize's class for Maths is PARTIAL (teacher-evidence-only): all 3 in-hand guides are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand, and the three *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* webs (Lower, Middle and Upper Division) sit at exact link only, unread.

## Brazil added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Brazil's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDF — **1 book, a pupil textbook: *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (SEED-PR), the Paraná state public textbook for Ensino Médio (Grades 10–12), in Portuguese. There is no teacher guide in the Brazil Maths set in hand, so every Brazil row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Brazil counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 128 teaching items. The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher = 1” is correct: books.json (grade-10/gov/brazil) and textbooks.tsv each carry exactly one Brazil Mathematics entry, and phase3-push-state.json lists the file as pushed. The staging folder for the book is empty, so there was no local copy to read, and the authenticated GitHub asset route failed in this pass (the mid-size-books release's asset listing repeatedly disconnected through the proxy, and the asset's download URL answered 404) — the GitHub copy's existence and size were therefore NOT verified here, and a library-side check of that asset is owed (a route failure, not a negative finding). The book was instead read from its own recorded source_url — the Wayback capture banked by the 2026-10-22 CDX prep (capture 20240421222141) — which landed at exactly the prep's expected 6,379,928 B, with 240 pages and the correct cover and catalogue block; the book read is certainly this book. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Four book errors found in the read (placements E1–E4) are carried corrected on the rows they touch — in the sequences-and-series (E1), ratio-and-proportion (E2), coordinate-geometry (E3), barycentres (E4) and loci (E4) lessons; two further misprints are recorded, not taught (R5: the risk exponent typeset once as 0.2525 for 0.2528, printed p. 58 — noted on the item 28 row in the logarithms-and-exponentials lesson; R6: glucose printed “C6A12O6”, printed p. 35, a chemistry misprint), and the Aristarchus bound passage (printed p. 169, record item R7) could not be confirmed either way as extracted and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the four section introductions and the general presentation (framing and history), the context essays and debates that carry no new maths, the biographical and historical passages except where the passage itself teaches a method, the “Obras Consultadas” reference lists after every chapter, and the blank “Anotações” pages — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item (all from the pupil textbook) from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (pupil textbook, Ensino Médio — Grades 10–12) — Brazil, Secretaria de Estado da Educação do Paraná (SEED-PR), Curitiba, 2nd edition 2006, ISBN 85-85380-39-X; 6,379,928 bytes, 240 PDF pp., MD5 e3dac71a6d075dd142282376da6fa785. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Read from the banked Wayback capture of the book's recorded source_url at the exact recorded size; the GitHub release-asset copy could not be verified in this pass (route failure — a library-side asset check is owed). The catalogue block's “p. 216” is the 1st-edition pagination and does not match this file's 240 pages (record item R1); all citations use the actual printed folios. Folha 6's chapter heading (printed p. 77) adds “Corrupção & Política –” to the contents-list title (record item R2) — the same chapter. Folhas 9 and 10 are filed by the book inside its Funções section although their content is trigonometry (record item R3); the placements follow the content.

The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The Class 1 activity book's Reproducible Pages (printed pp. 75–80) and Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (pp. 81–100) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The pupil Chapter 9 pages open at Lesson 3 (Interpreting Bar Graphs) — no pupil Lessons 1–2 for that chapter appear in the file; the guide's parallel survey and data-collection lessons stand as teacher evidence instead. Book errors in the Class 2 book are carried corrected: its imprint prints the copyright year as "20234", read as 2023 as on its guide, and its Chapter 6 Lesson 6 tells pupils to draw a cylinder and label its base and apex — a cylinder has no apex; the apex belongs to the cone in the same lesson. The Class 3 textbook's Chapter Overviews, Chapter Reviews and course-objectives/assessment front matter are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The Class IV textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class V textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class VI textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. Its contents numbers two different sections 6.2.2 ("Bisectors" and "EXPLORE: Sorting Quadrilaterals"); the second is cited with the duplication named.
